## NUMERICAL REVIEW

# Numerical formula alignment — after the movement

This is an informed editorial pass, not a blind cold read. Read the supplied
formula and full movement, then report target, observed result, method, coverage
and uncertainty. Keep numerical alignment separate from reader scores.

- Sentence mean, median, population standard deviation; shares ≤5 and ≥40 words.
- Written paragraph mean/median and actual scene/section boundaries. Compare with
  the requested targets, but do not equate typography with ASR pause segmentation.
- Flesch Reading Ease and Flesch-Kincaid grade. Identify the sentence tokenizer and
  syllable estimator; proper names/system terms can affect estimates.
- Progression vocabulary hits per 10,000 words, and placement by equal word-count
  book thirds once available. Define a project-specific lexicon before counting;
  do not borrow the source novel's names. An incomplete book cannot confirm the
  full front-loading ratio. Report provisional cumulative coverage instead.
- Lead development moments and supporting-cast distribution: cite actual choices
  or realizations and distinguish manually identified beats from regex proxies.
- POV word share: identify viewpoint segments, not the most frequently mentioned
  name. Track the lead and secondary viewpoints against the book-level allocation.
- Secondary signals: -ly words, reporting verbs, combat terms, fillers and repeated
  phrases. Publish lexicons and counting definitions. A suffix count is not proof
  that every matched word is an adverb. Preserve optional/non-target distinctions.

Use reproducible counts when tooling exists. Otherwise mark a metric UNMEASURED;
never estimate a precise score by intuition. Save the method or command with the
report so later runs use the same definitions. Inspect ASR reference limitations
without discarding the owner's chosen drafting targets.

Locate meaningful departures, especially uniform sentence lengths, missing short
beats, reading level drift, dense paragraphs, late teaching or a coequal second
lead replacing the requested POV shape. Consolidate the most important repairs
with continuity findings into the existing bounded author repair. Protect exact
owner text and successful scenes. Do not iterate indefinitely to hit decimals or
hide irreconcilable targets. Bring consequential formula conflicts to the owner.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — BOOK_MAP.md

# Kindling 2680 — Book One map

Status: drafting map. Opening locks resolved 2026-10-08; prose run authorized.

## 1. Book promise

Kiva Rowan opened all three known doors at thirteen and spent four years classified
below what any one of them might have earned alone. In 2680 she enters Meridian's
collegiate competition system, the regional feeder for professional rift companies.
Across one season, she must turn breadth into reliable control, keep friendships
strained by ranking and recruitment, learn the ashcraft that powers her world, and
answer a real rift event where category matters less than whether the old Home Line
method still works. Beneath that season, Kiva's recurring Unroofed Sky fever dream
shows Glory users converging as the planetary filter disappears.

## 2. Ending promise

Kiva completes the collegiate season, earns a public place without receiving a
finished explanation, and deliberately combines the doors once under rules the
reader understands. The win costs something that cannot be reset by rank. The disk
is recognized as belonging to the old bearer line; a professional company makes a
bounded recruitment offer; and evidence from the emergency shows the filter trend is
accelerating. A second historical account confirms that part of Kiva's fever dream
predates her, without explaining it. Book One closes regionally. Book Two's national
competition, energy-industry conflict, and shared-prophecy problem are opened, not
substituted for the ending.

## 3. Numerical and structural shape

- One planned historical prologue precedes Chapter 1. It must establish the EMP,
  fallout, failed restoration, first Kindlings, ash's gradual industrial adoption,
  radiation-safe nuclear recovery, ash-nuclear synthesis, distributed reactors,
  flight, Glory industry, devoted school orders, and rebuilding through ten human
  scenes without answering the reserved cause of the Fall. Its final snapshot shows
  Kiva's anomalous Kindling and first Unroofed Sky fever dream at thirteen in 2676.
  Chapter One jumps four years to the 2680 collegiate season.
- Six movements, eight chapters each; 48 chapters working total.
- Working length 270,000–315,000 words, adjusted for actual scene need.
- Approximately 87% protagonist viewpoint/focus and 13% purposeful secondary
  focus across four or five characters.
- O'Connor numerical prose targets apply: 14.6-word mean, 11-word median, about
  27.7% sentences of five words or fewer, about 3.3% of forty words or more,
  short paragraphs, accessible approximately seventh-grade readability, and
  front-loaded progression teaching.
- Major fights receive developed tactical space. No required fight count replaces
  story need, but every movement has a physical test, field exercise, or deliberate
  quiet reason.

## 4. Six movements

| Movement | Entry pressure | Learning / relationship engine | Action and turn | Exit state |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — Three Doors** (Ch. 1–8) | Four years after her anomalous Kindling and fever dream, Kiva enters collegiate placement with partial training but no safe combined classification | Establish teams, standings, scholarships, pro recruitment, Mara's ash-nuclear repair work, the three traditions, and rank versus control | A placement trial combining combat, ashcraft, and rescue fails in an original way; Kiva protects someone but worsens her mark; the dream recurs as a brief physical interruption rather than guidance | Provisional lowest-division roster place; Orla takes accountable sponsorship; a pro scout notices the disk only after Kiva earns attention |
| **2 — The Crooked Lane** (Ch. 9–16) | Kiva trains inside a college system built for one-door specialists and is treated as both weak and dangerous | Declared roles, reasons ledger, one obstacle/three answers, ash-cell tuning, rivalry, humor, and team identity | First sanctioned collegiate meet: one-door limits inside a changing team lane. A useful loss proves control matters more than access | Kiva earns continued roster standing; Noa records output and ash efficiency that do not fit the institutional model |
| **3 — First Season** (Ch. 17–24) | The circuit accelerates; friends and rival advance toward recruitment on paths Kiva cannot copy | Two-door transitions and blended ashcraft are attempted and fail; professional footage reveals the field ceiling | Developed learning fight against an adaptive rival plus an ashcraft event whose failure threatens the arena grid | First deliberate two-door sequence works briefly; the disk warms after; another Unroofed Sky fragment appears but supplies no tactic |
| **4 — What the Record Lost** (Ch. 25–32) | Public results make the anomaly searchable; founder histories, ash patents, and old fever accounts disagree | Noa, Orla, and Hollis pursue different evidence. Supporting cast makes independent placement and recruitment choices | A supervised professional shadow shift becomes a real Riftspawn call. Competition habits fail until declared roles and reasons return | Kiva sees professional rift fighting without spectacle's safety net, learns the disk was released to a bearer, and finds a pre-Kiva Unroofed Sky fragment |
| **5 — The Regional Measure** (Ch. 33–40) | Colleges, utilities, industry houses, school orders, and professional companies want ownership, exclusion, or proof | Friendship fractures/reforms around risk, credit, sponsorship, and ash recovery; Kiva trains one controlled three-door transition with abort conditions | Long team/bracket/ashcraft sequence is interrupted by filter instability; rival and teammates adapt instead of becoming props | The three-door configuration is publicly real but not mastered. Kiva permanently loses fine sensation in two fingers; no ranking erases it |
| **6 — Why It Is Crooked** (Ch. 41–48) | A consequential rift emergency hits the collegiate district and ash grid; students and professionals must cooperate | Earlier drills, ash shutdown practice, and each supporting character's independent skill become the response plan | Kiva sequences all three doors to stabilize a failing induction/rift boundary while professionals and peers own combat, rescue, and grid tasks | Emergency answered; regional season closes; disk recognition, a bounded pro offer, and measured filter acceleration open the national Book Two |

## 5. Progression design

### First third

Teach the three doors, rank/skill distinction, soul/ash limits, ash induction,
magic/ash blending, collegiate rules, professional recruitment, classification, and
institutional stakes aggressively. Let failed attempts and demonstrations carry the
exposition. Kiva is not secretly powerful; breadth creates control problems, fatigue,
poor efficiency, and team risk before it becomes an advantage.

### Middle third

Reduce vocabulary repetition and let relationships, competition, and tactics carry
the system. Two-door use appears only after single-door discipline is visible.

### Final third

Surface rules at true turning points. The first intentional three-door sequence is
short, costly, and planted. It proves configuration, not mastery or Glory.

## 6. Plant/payoff ledger

| Plant | Payoff |
|---|---|
| Three doors open; classification cannot settle | Public controlled sequence in Movement 6 |
| Crooked post retained without a clear reason | Founder record explains why; protagonist applies the principle rather than worshiping the object |
| Disk warms only after Kiva's first correct deliberate sequence | Orla and Noa recognize the appointed bearer line, not a cosmic origin |
| Rival's strong single-door and ashcraft model | Rival adapts and owns an essential climax task Kiva cannot replace |
| Friend's envy or outside goal | Friendship survives only after protagonist stops treating support as automatic |
| Home Line field guide doubts school metrics | Real emergency proves both metrics and judgment are needed |
| Conflicting copies of the reasons ledger | Missing reason exposes institutional drift and guides a repair |
| Filter measurement anomaly | Final emergency produces credible evidence of acceleration, not a full realm break |
| Ash induction powers Mara's shop, the colleges, and the arena | Grid failure in Movement 6 has understood mechanisms, shutdowns, and civic stakes |
| Professional standings count rescue, closure, infrastructure, and clean ash | Kiva's first pro offer recognizes judgment rather than kill count |
| Kiva sees converging Glory figures and an absent sky in the 2676 fever dream | Historical fragment in Movement 4 confirms shared imagery; Books Two and Three widen the convergence |
| Prologue shows radiation rotations, nuclear-ash synthesis, compact cores, and flight | Every later grid, arena, rescue, and industrial conflict rests on understood historical mechanisms |

## 7. Movement One packet rule

The names and six opening locks in `STATE_LEDGER.md` are resolved. Movement One now
receives entry state, pressure, boundaries, output paths, and the whole eight-chapter
run—never forty-eight miniature chapter contracts.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — STATE_LEDGER.md

# Kindling 2680 opening state ledger

Status: opening locks resolved 2026-10-08. Ready for the prologue and first
eight-chapter continuous prose run.

## Prologue and opening day

- Year 2680, during the six-hundredth-anniversary season of the Fall.
- The prologue's final historical snapshot is 2676: **Kiva Rowan**, thirteen,
  reaches the Kindling uninjured and opens all three known doors.
- Chapter One begins four years later. Kiva is seventeen and seeking collegiate
  placement for the 2680 season.
- Kiva lives with her aunt and legal guardian, **Mara Rowan**, in Meridian. Mara
  repairs municipal instruments and reconciles their paper records.
- The Satori disk reached Kiva through a sealed Home Line appointment by its prior
  bearer, not through blood descent. Kiva knows it is old and entrusted; she does
  not know its full public history or origin.
- Meridian's schools expect one first door and have procedures for unusual strength,
  but no accepted process for three simultaneous traditions.

## Knowledge boundaries

- General public: knows the Fall, Kindling, ranks, schools, Riftspawn, and ordinary
  filter language as history/science appropriate to the final world-state choice.
- Students: know institutional versions of Home Line history, not necessarily the
  surviving founder records.
- Orla Dane: knows the disk family and at least one suppressed prior anomaly.
- Kiva: does not know the disk's true origin, the full filter theory, or why
  all three doors opened.
- Kiva remembers the 2676 fever dream in sensory fragments: Glory-level figures
  converging, the sky/filter absent, pure presence pressing into the world, and every
  ash-lit thing responding. She does not know the later name **Unroofed Sky**, whether
  the dream predicts anything, or that older Glory accounts overlap it.
- Orla knows there are disputed historical fever accounts but does not begin Book One
  with proof that Kiva's dream matches them.
- Reader: sees evidence before explanation; no prologue solves the mystery.

## Physical and progression state

- All three doors open at Kindling, but none is mastered.
- The event leaves three fine, asymmetrical heat-lines from Kiva's sternum toward
  her left shoulder. They remain visible and become tender when the doors compete.
- The disk warms once, only after Kiva completes the doors in a correct deliberate
  sequence. It never instructs her or grants technique.
- The disk can amplify or carry the Unroofed Sky fever dream, but it cannot trigger
  on demand, answer questions, or supply a combat/engineering solution.
- Initial classification is incomplete or contradictory, not a secret top rank.

## Social state

- Kiva has a real home/family obligation in Mara's repair-and-records work.
- Anwen Pryce, Perrin Cade, and Kellan Renn have independent placements and
  goals before meeting the protagonist.
- Institutions have defensible reasons to fear a category they cannot safely test.

## Institutional and world locks

- Campus compact: **the Meridian Compact**.
- Mixed-path college: **the Crooked Line School**.
- Resilience/body college: **Stonehand Hall**.
- Medical, Ward, Sustain, and ash-engineering college: **Wren House**.
- Collegiate competition: **the Meridian Collegiate Circuit**; final regional
  event: **the Regional Measure**. Events include individual Measures, team lanes,
  ashcraft trials, rescue/extraction problems, and controlled Riftspawn engagements.
- Above college are **chartered rift companies**: salaried professional teams that
  fight Riftspawn, close or contain rifts, rescue civilians, protect infrastructure,
  and recover ash. Standings reward closure, lives saved, protected infrastructure,
  clean ash, and rule compliance—not kills alone.
- Book One is regional. Book Two expands toward national competition and company
  politics. Book Three becomes international as the filter and ash grid destabilize.
- Mature hardened regional technology and scheduled wormhole stations exist. Travel
  is expensive and bounded; interplanetary normality does not.
- Classification uses physical instruments, projected tables, and human rulings.
  There is no private retinal or software-like status overlay.

## Ash-electric civilization and Glory

- Refined ash is the dominant finite energy source. **Ash induction** converts a
  controlled ash reaction into ordinary current for lights, pumps, heat,
  communications, medicine, workshops, transit, arenas, and rift sensing.
- Ash does not create free energy. Cells are graded, metered, consumed, reconciled,
  and vulnerable to contamination or runaway.
- **Ashcraft** blends magic and ash: a trained soul supplies pattern, intention,
  and control; ownerless ash supplies most of the fuel. Different traditions shape,
  contain, conduct, or sustain the reaction differently.
- Modern cells continue running after a Kindled operator starts and tunes them, but
  still require maintenance, replacement ash, and emergency shutdown.
- At **Glory**, a rare master can impress stable patterns at civic or industrial
  scale. Glory does not make ash infinite; it expands safe throughput, duration,
  precision, and the complexity of blended systems.
- That capability produces ash-power industrial dynasties as well as school orders
  whose masters pursue near-monastic devotion to one tradition. Some orders reject
  corporate ownership; some industry houses sponsor them; some Glory masters move
  between both worlds.
- Pro rift companies feed the ash grid. This creates prestige and public service,
  but also incentives to overhunt, hide risk, manipulate ash grades, or protect
  wealthy districts first.

## First-season lasting cost

During the high-stakes regional sequence Kiva will permanently lose fine sensation
in the first two fingers of her left hand. Healing closes the injury but does not
restore exact touch. The cost is planted before it occurs and changes how she grips,
trains, and asks for help. Rank cannot erase it.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md

# Kindling 2680 — reconciled planning bible

Status: drafting bible. `INHERITED` means established in the present Kindled canon.
`LOCKED 2680` marks the opening-book choices delegated by the owner on 2026-10-08.
This file does not silently promote the older storyboard's claims.

## Timeline

- **2080 — Fall (`INHERITED`):** stratospheric detonations cause the visible EMP
  collapse; their relationship to filter damage remains uncertain.
- **~2090 onward (`INHERITED`):** Mars-recovered disk activation, unstable
  wormholes, Riftspawn, and human Kindling begin.
- **2150 (`INHERITED`):** the present Kindled trilogy and first Home Line.
- **2680 (`PLANNED`):** six-hundredth anniversary of the Fall; roughly 530 years of
  institutional growth from Meridian's first lanes.

## Power invariants

- One underlying source is approached through three known traditions: Ember,
  rift-granted magic, and disciplined internal cultivation.
- The soul is what fills. Ash is ownerless fuel left by Riftspawn; it is finite.
- Kindled → Ember → Flame → Fire → Torch → Blaze → Glory remains the human rank
  ladder. Skill/control and rank/breadth remain separate axes.
- Glory stops ordinary aging through the shedding mechanism already recorded, but
  reaching it is rare and cannot be granted, bought, or stolen.
- Glory also allows a master to hold larger, longer, and more intricate ashcraft
  patterns. It improves throughput, stability, duration, and precision; it does not
  make ash infinite or remove the need for equipment, workers, ledgers, and shutdowns.
- Satori is both realization and the disk name. The disk's true origin is never
  explained in-manuscript.
- Riftspawn expressions echo worked powers without a person home to direct them.
- The filter may be failing, but characters distinguish measurement, theory, myth,
  and proof.

## The Unroofed Sky (`LOCKED SERIES PROPHECY`)

The series uses a recurring fever dream, not a reliable spoken prophecy. Across
centuries, a small number of high-rank practitioners have recorded overlapping
fragments. Later schools call the collected pattern **the Unroofed Sky**.

- The dream shows Glory-level people arriving from different directions and
  traditions until their fields touch. Whether they gather to prevent the break,
  cause it, claim it, or survive it is not revealed.
- Above them, the filter does not crack wider. It disappears. The planet stands in
  unmediated **Glory presence**: immense, intelligent-feeling pressure and possibility,
  but not a speaking god and not automatically hostile.
- Every ash cell, reactor lattice, living door, and rift responds at once. Some
  systems brighten, some fail, some change category. The dream never supplies a
  technical solution.
- Kiva's three-door Kindling includes the clearest known version, apparently carried
  or amplified by the Satori disk. The disk may preserve earlier contact, respond to
  future resonance, or both. Book One does not settle which.
- The vision recurs in pieces as Kiva advances. She cannot summon it for answers.
  Physical fever, ash overload, filter instability, or close contact with Glory can
  trigger it, and each recurrence has a bodily cost.
- Independent Glory masters and archival accounts eventually prove Kiva is not the
  only dreamer. Industrial houses, school orders, professional companies, nations,
  and religious movements interpret the pattern differently.
- Book Two reveals the shared record and makes the dream a national/international
  political fact. Book Three brings the Glory users together and fulfills the fixed
  event: complete planetary filter loss and enduring pure Glory presence. The
  characters' choices determine what that collision becomes.

The fixed ending is the loss of the filter, not a predetermined winner, savior,
battle, or moral interpretation. Do not use the dream to excuse unearned power or
replace evidence, training, logistics, and choice.

## Meridian after 530 years (`PLANNED`)

Meridian has grown from hardened medical enclave into a regional learning and
response center. The recommended institutional shape is one campus/compact with
several traditions, not a single harmonious academy:

1. **Crooked Line School (`LOCKED 2680`):** mixed-door field method descended from
   Toren, Rook, and the declared-role lane. It preserves method rather than turning
   a founder's name into a correctness claim.
2. **Stonehand Hall (`LOCKED 2680`):** Resilience/body discipline, contact, load,
   safe failure, and recovery.
3. **Wren House (`LOCKED 2680`):** Ward, Sustain, medicine, ash depth,
   ash engineering, rotation, radiation recall, and abort-line ethics.
4. **Home Line (`PLANNED` retained name):** non-chartered or semi-chartered field
   response. It tests whether school methods survive real terrain.

Together they operate through **the Meridian Compact**. Their sanctioned collegiate
season is **the Meridian Collegiate Circuit**, ending in **the Regional Measure**.
It is a major public institution with teams, scholarships, standings, recruiters,
broadcast coverage, and real consequences. Events test dueling, declared-role lanes,
rescue/extraction, ashcraft, and controlled Riftspawn response.

The collegiate system feeds **chartered rift companies**: professional salaried teams
licensed to answer live rifts, fight Riftspawn, rescue civilians, protect
infrastructure, and recover clean ash. Public standings count closure, lives saved,
damage prevented, usable ash, and violations. Home Line remains the professional
tradition most suspicious of spectacle and most insistent that reasons and civilian
outcomes stay in the record.

Callie's boundary practice and Dessa/Senna's records are embedded across these
institutions rather than requiring a fourth founder cult.

## Technology and travel

- Mature hardened technology, medical/Ember synthesis, reliable regional
  communications, and ash-electric infrastructure are `LOCKED 2680`.
- A rebuilt network connects established regional wormhole stations on schedules.
  Transit is costly, capacity-limited, and vulnerable to rift conditions.
- Interplanetary/intergalactic normality is not part of the opening book.
- Classification instruments measure door behavior through physical rigs, projected
  tables, and accountable human rulings. There is no retinal HUD or private status
  overlay.

## Ashcraft and electrical power (`LOCKED 2680`)

Centuries of work have turned ash from carried healer fuel into the rebuilt world's
principal finite energy commodity.

- **Ash induction:** refined ash sits in a warded cell. A trained operator impresses
  a stable magical pattern on it; the induction assembly converts the controlled
  release into ordinary electrical current. Once tuned, a modern cell runs without
  continuous soul expenditure, but never without finite fuel, maintenance, metering,
  or risk.
- **Magic supplies form; ash supplies fuel.** A soul cannot be replaced by ash because
  ash has no owner, judgment, or intent. Ash cannot safely do complex work without a
  pattern held by a person or preserved in a carefully built device.
- **Traditions remain distinct:** Edge-derived work defines conductive paths and
  precise release; Ward-derived work contains reaction and redirects force;
  cultivated/internal work holds resonance, distributes load, and prevents collapse.
  Composite devices normally require teams or inherited patterns. Three-door access
  gives Kiva possibility, not instant mastery.
- **Uses:** lighting, water and waste pumps, heating, refrigeration, communications,
  medical machinery, tools, transit, station gates, arenas, and rift sensing.
  Critical services retain mechanical and hardened backups.
- **Failure modes:** contaminated ash, false grading, pattern drift, runaway heat,
  operator backwash, grid damage, and rift attraction. Every serious device has a
  meter, a shutdown path, and a responsible ledger.
- **Glory scale:** a Glory master can stabilize industrial patterns that lesser
  practitioners can only maintain in teams or for short intervals. This has produced
  powerful industrial houses—titans whose refineries, grids, transit works, and
  professional companies shape governments—as well as near-monastic school orders
  devoted to perfecting one discipline across a lifetime.
- Industry and devotion are not clean moral opposites. Some houses exploit schools;
  some fund public infrastructure; some orders hoard knowledge; some resist monopoly;
  and a few Glory masters belong credibly to both worlds.
- **Geographic ladder:** Book One remains regional around Meridian. Book Two widens
  to national circuits, intercity grids, and company charters. Book Three becomes
  international as rival grids, schools, Glory masters, and rift companies must
  cooperate under accelerating filter failure.

## Energy revolution after Meridian (`LOCKED 2680 HISTORY`)

The decisive technological leap occurred during the first century after the first
Home Line, then compounded for more than four hundred years.

1. **Ash as emergency fuel:** Meridian-era practitioners used ash mainly for healing,
   training, lamps, and survival work. Its finite nature and dangerous grading were
   understood before its industrial potential.
2. **Radiation return:** Sustain-trained Kindled learned to remove active radiation
   burden from living workers while medicine treated exposure and fixed damage.
   They could enter zones ordinary crews could not, recover records and components,
   and work in short, measured rotations. This never made radiation harmless or
   reversed tissue already destroyed.
3. **Nuclear recovery:** engineers combined ordinary shielding, remote machinery,
   disciplined rotations, ash containment patterns, and Glory-scale oversight to
   reopen surviving nuclear sites and build safer compact reactors. Ward does not
   move radiation; the breakthrough is containment, control, monitoring, and safe
   human access—not a quiet rewrite of the old power rules.
4. **Tamar Vey's question:** an itinerant Home Line teacher and storyteller visits
   an early recovery workshop whose engineers keep trying to make ash burn longer.
   With an ordinary bowl, a conductor, a measured pinch of ash, and a short-held
   pattern, Tamar demonstrates that ash can preserve an instruction after the
   operator releases it. The lesson is conceptual: the reactor's fire already knows
   how to make heat; perhaps ash should hold the rules around the fire. Tamar does
   not design the reactor or hand civilization a finished device. Engineers spend
   years turning that question into safe, reproducible work.
5. **Ash-nuclear synthesis:** ash patterns stabilize reaction geometry, manage heat
   and load, and improve electrical conversion. Nuclear fuel supplies dependable
   baseline energy; ash supplies control, high-density response, and compactness.
   Both fuels remain finite. Waste and failure still matter.
6. **Distributed power:** licensed Glory-impressed patterns allow factory-built
   small reactors and ash cells to run homes, buildings, farms, hospitals, and
   workshops without a Glory master standing beside each one. Local technicians
   maintain them; public ledgers track fuel and failures.
7. **Displacement:** within roughly a century of Meridian, ash induction and compact
   nuclear-ash systems displaced fossil fuels and large solar fields as primary
   energy. Legacy wind, water, solar, combustion, and mechanical systems remain as
   local backups rather than vanishing from knowledge.
8. **Flight and advanced equipment:** ash-electric lift craft, personal lift rigs for
   suitably trained Kindled, powered armor/tools, precision medicine, climate control,
   high-speed fabrication, and hardened communications become ordinary enough to
   reshape cities. Personal flight remains skill- and fuel-limited, not universal.
9. **Far-future society:** four more centuries of refinement make 2680 materially
   beyond most pre-Fall dreams. Its abundance is real but uneven. Grid access, clean
   ash, reactor licensing, professional protection, and Glory-pattern ownership are
   central political and class questions.

Tamar's role is remembered unevenly. Some accounts make him an inventor; others call
him a fraud who happened to ask the right question. Canonically he is mortal, human,
original to this world, and catalytic rather than omniscient. The science belongs to
the generations who test, fail, record, and build after him.

## Historical artifacts

- **The Satori disk (`INHERITED` → `LOCKED 2680 survival`):** nine grooves; released
  through an appointed bearer line, not blood descent. It warms once after Kiva's
  first correct deliberate three-door sequence. It does not teach or speak.
- **The reasons ledger (`PLANNED survival`):** preserved in copies with disagreements.
  A missing or edited reason is a story problem, not proof the founders were wrong.
- **The crooked post (`PLANNED survival`):** retained in one ceremonial/training
  lane because the road is not straight. Other schools may mock or straighten it.
- **Home Line records (`PLANNED survival`):** case files of exercises, injuries,
  costs, recovery, repeat attempts, and reasons.

## Filter and Glory horizon

The filter's failure remains a theory supported by increasingly abnormal rifts and
generation-scale measurements. Book One proves acceleration, not cosmology. If the
2680 trilogy later reaches full integration, Glory becomes minimum viable contact
with the larger realm rather than a final victory. Populated worlds and the next
ladder remain `HORIZON` until the owner approves their concrete form.

## Guardrails

- Do not bring a present Kindled character onstage as an immortal unless the owner
  separately approves that character having reached Glory.
- Do not turn disputed founder history into a secret villain rewrite.
- Do not make the protagonist powerful because the disk grants finished skill.
- Do not copy source-novel names, terminology, scene order, signature dialogue, or
  character analogs from the research.
- Every climax capability must be demonstrated and priced earlier.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — PROLOGUE_PLAN.md

# Kindling 2680 — prologue plan

Status: authorized for drafting with Movement One. This plan describes architecture,
purpose, and boundaries for the required historical prologue.

## 1. Purpose

`BOOK_MAP.md` §3 already requires one historical prologue before Chapter 1,
sitting outside the six eight-chapter movements, carrying a cold reader from
the 2080 Fall to the 2680 anniversary without resolving the reserved cosmology.
This plan supplies that architecture.

The prologue's job is substantial but disciplined: let a reader who has never opened
a Kindled book feel the 2080 Fall, the recovery, the first century's energy
revolution, and six hundred years of consequence as human events, then show Kiva's
anomalous Kindling and first Unroofed Sky fever dream four years before the
collegiate story begins. It is not a world-building appendix and not a
summary of the present Kindled trilogy's plot. A reader of the present trilogy
should recognize the institutions (Meridian, the Home Line, the crooked lane)
arriving as history; a reader who has never heard of them should still follow
the human thread without needing to.

## 2. Placement and scale

- Precedes Chapter 1. Does not count as one of the six movements and is not
  itself subject to the O'Connor six-or-more-chapter floor.
- Scene-led throughout. No snapshot is a lecture, a textbook paragraph, or an
  encyclopedia entry rendered as prose. Every snapshot is one person, in one
  place, on one day, doing something concrete.
- Target scale: eleven dated, scene-led snapshots, usually 900–1,600 words each.
  The expected prologue is approximately 11,000–17,000 words. It may be longer than
  a normal chapter because the owner wants the energy and social transformation
  understood rather than summarized, but every scene must turn on one person's
  immediate problem and choice.
- Each snapshot is explicitly dated (a year or a "N years after the Fall"
  marker) so the reader always knows how much time has passed since the last
  one, without needing a narrator to say so directly.

## 3. Canon boundaries

**May do:**
- Show the 2080 EMP's visible effects (light in the sky, total hardware
  failure, immediate social collapse) through one household's night.
- Show the failed restoration decade (2080–2090) through one person's labor,
  per `UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md` (book-01) owner ruling: a small number of
  pre-hardened medical/research facilities survive intact, most
  infrastructure does not.
- Show the Mars-disk activation, the first unstable wormholes, the first
  Riftspawn, and the first Kindlings as lived, frightening, un-named events —
  no character in these early snapshots has rank vocabulary, a classification
  system, or the word "Kindling" itself until it is plausible for that word to
  exist.
- Show the slow, centuries-long institutionalization of door schools, medical
  practice, and the Home Line's field tradition — including, explicitly, the
  moment or stretch where a later generation simplifies or loses a founder's
  reason while keeping the founder's method. This directly serves the
  series promise already stated in `SERIES_MAP.md`: *"institutions preserved
  the founders' methods but simplified away the reason those methods
  worked."*
- Show ash expanding from scarce healing fuel into industrial energy through
  attempts, accidents, ledgers, and engineering choices. Include radiation-safe
  Kindled work, recovered nuclear knowledge, ash-nuclear synthesis, compact reactors,
  household power, flight, and the social rise of industrial houses and devoted
  schools.
- Show that Sustain-trained workers can shed active radiation burden under strict
  limits; they are not immune, fixed tissue damage remains, and Ward still does not
  move radiation.
- Let the Unroofed Sky appear first as a fragment in an older Glory practitioner's
  private record, then fully in Kiva's fever dream. The overlap must be visible to
  the reader but not fully explained to Kiva.
- Let characters across every era hold and voice theories about the Fall's
  cause and the filter — a nation-state exchange, a deliberate reset, later
  (post-Homura only, see §5) the failing-filter hypothesis — without the
  narration confirming any of them.
- End in 2676 with Kiva's three-door Kindling and the crooked post. Chapter One
  then opens four years later in the 2680 collegiate season.

**Must not do:**
- Put Rook on the page as a witness to the 2080 EMP or any pre-Homura event.
  Current canon places him generations later (born well after the Fall,
  Kindled at thirteen, recruited to Homura at fourteen). No snapshot may
  use him, his mother, or any named Book One/Two character as a 2080-era
  figure.
- Prove, confirm, or narratively settle the filter cosmology, the Makers, the
  Mother/Father myth, the disk's true origin, or the first cause of the Fall.
  The prologue may show belief, measurement, and memory. It may not show
  authorial confirmation of any of them.
- Reuse named systems, terminology, or scene sequence from the Iron Prince
  research pool. Every invented figure, place, and phrase in the prologue is
  original to this series.
- Let any pre-Homura-era snapshot originate or anticipate the failing-filter
  theory. Per `UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md` (book-01), that theory is first held by
  "Homura-era theorists" — it cannot appear earlier in this timeline without
  rewriting already-locked canon.
- Resolve whether the detonations and the filter damage are connected. Keep
  both the nation-state/deliberate-reset ambiguity and the filter-trigger
  question exactly as open as `UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md` (book-01) leaves them.
- Turn the energy history into a triumphalist lecture. Every advancement has fuel,
  labor, failure, ownership, safety, and distribution consequences.
- Exhaust the Unroofed Sky mystery. The prologue establishes the repeating images;
  it does not prove whether they are memory, prophecy, resonance, or manipulation.

## 4. The shared physical thread: the copied record

Per the owner's pairing instruction (postscript = intention, prologue =
inheritance), this plan adopts the same lineage object the Book Three
postscript plants: a hand-copied record — originating, in Book Three, as a
copy of Dessa's reasons ledger that leaves Meridian in at least one direction
(see `book-03-wellspring/POSTSCRIPT_PLAN.md` §4).

The prologue does not need to assert an unbroken chain of custody back to that
exact document — six hundred years and at least one dark age make a literal
unbroken chain implausible and unnecessary. Instead, the thread recurs as a
*class* of object across the prologue's own snapshots: someone, in nearly
every era shown, keeps a hand-written record of what happened and why,
because nothing else will. That habit is the thread. Its specific late
appearance — a damaged, partial, or disputed page whose attributed reason
("because the road isn't straight") no longer makes sense to the people still
following the rule it describes — is this prologue's closing image, and the
opening of Book One's Movement 4 plant ("conflicting copies of the reasons
ledger... missing reason exposes institutional drift," `BOOK_MAP.md` §6).

This satisfies the pairing without requiring either file to depend on the
other's prose: a reader of only the prologue sees a believable, self-contained
pattern of record-keeping surviving collapse and recovery; a reader of both
books recognizes exactly where that habit began.

## 5. Recommended snapshot architecture

Eleven snapshots, each with an explicit causal/temporal handoff to the next.
Order, dating, and function are the load-bearing elements; exact word count
and point-of-view character are drafting decisions.

| # | Date | Human anchor | What it shows | Guardrail |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2080, the night of the Fall | One household in a tech-zone city | Light in the stratosphere, no felt blast, then every powered thing in the house dies at once; fear and confusion, not explanation. One adult in the household begins a written log "because somebody should write down what happened." | No cause stated or implied; two or more characters may already disagree about what they saw |
| 2 | ~2085, the failed restoration decade | A technician or caregiver near one of the rare surviving hardened facilities | The decade's reality: most infrastructure gone, a few hardened medical/research sites functioning, enormous local inequality in who gets care. The log-keeping habit recurs independently — a second person, unconnected to snapshot 1, starts their own record for the same plain reason | Do not resolve why these specific sites survived beyond the owner's stated reason (hardened by design, for unrelated purposes) |
| 3 | ~2095, first wormhole / first Riftspawn / first Kindling | An ordinary adult or adolescent, unnamed or lightly named, with no door vocabulary yet | A wormhole tears open nearby; something comes through; the point-of-view character's hands or body do something that saves someone and cannot be explained by anything they knew beforehand. No one present has words like "Kindling," "Ember," or "rank" — those words do not exist yet. The scene may be rendered partly through a fragment of someone's written log rather than full dramatization, to compress distance without summarizing it | This is the series' own origin-of-power moment; keep it discovery-shaped (accident under real stakes), not an instructor explaining a system |
| 4 | ~2135–2150, Homura and the first Home Line era | A brief, respectful record-keeper outside the named trilogy cast | Ash remains scarce medical and field fuel. The first reasons records, radiation recall, and filter theory appear as hard-won methods rather than mature industry | Do not dramatize Rook, Vera, Toren, Callie, Jab, Dessa, or any named Book One/Two figure; show their era through records and consequence |
| 5 | ~2170, the radiation return | A Sustain worker and nuclear engineer entering a dead power site in measured rotation | Kindled workers recall active radiation burden from living bodies, recover reactor records/components, and prove access is possible. A worker aborts on time rather than becoming a martyr | No immunity, no repair of fixed damage, no Ward-moving-radiation shortcut; ordinary shielding, instruments, medicine, and finite ash remain essential |
| 6 | ~2182, Tamar Vey's lesson | Tamar Vey, an itinerant reasons teacher and storyteller, with an exhausted reactor engineer and a few workers | The engineer keeps asking how to make ash burn longer. Tamar uses a plain story and a small physical demonstration to separate fuel from instruction: ash can hold a shaped intention briefly after the person lets go. He leaves the engineer with the question of using ash to hold the rules around nuclear heat rather than replacing the heat | Original wise-teacher function only: Tamar is mortal, fallible, not the reactor's inventor, and not modeled in voice, identity, or mythology on an existing fictional character. The engineers must do the difficult work afterward |
| 7 | ~2195, the first stable ash-nuclear plant | The engineer shaped by Tamar's question, an operator, and a public observer during commissioning | Magic patterns stabilize containment and conversion while nuclear fuel supplies baseline heat. The first large output holds, then reveals a dangerous failure mode that becomes part of every later shutdown code | Both nuclear and ash fuel remain finite; no perpetual motion, erased waste, or effortless miniaturization |
| 8 | ~2245, one century after Meridian | A household technician installing an early licensed compact core | Household/building reactors, ash cells, pumps, heat, refrigeration, medicine, and fabrication make ordinary life exceed pre-Fall recovery goals. Fossil and large solar systems become secondary/backup rather than the main grid | Show price, licensing, maintenance, and unequal access alongside wonder; one century of advancement is earned through previous scenes |
| 9 | ~2360, the lift century | A collegiate pilot or professional rift fighter on an early ash-electric flight/rescue | Lift craft, personal flight rigs, powered tools, professional rift teams, and ash recovery reshape travel and combat. A professional chooses rescue over a richer ash haul | Flight is fuel- and skill-limited; professional standing counts closure, lives, infrastructure, clean ash, and violations |
| 10 | ~2600, Glory industry and school devotion | An archivist caught between a Glory industrial house and a monastic school order | Glory-impressed industrial patterns, vast companies, disciplined orders, and modern colleges exist. A reason is cut from a standard record for efficiency. Separately, the archivist finds an older Glory fever account describing the first shared images of the Unroofed Sky | Industry and devotion are not simple evil/good opposites; the dream is evidence of repetition, not proof of cause or outcome |
| 11 | 2676, four years before the anniversary | Thirteen-year-old Kiva and Mara Rowan | Ordinary preparation in a future city beyond pre-Fall dreams; Kiva's Kindling opens all three doors; instruments disagree; the heat-line mark appears; the disk amplifies the full Unroofed Sky dream. End on the crooked post outside the yard | Do not solve the anomaly or let the disk grant technique. Chapter One opens four years later in the 2680 collegiate season |

## 6. Transition logic into Chapter 1

- The final snapshot (2676) ends on the crooked post, not a thesis statement.
  Chapter One begins with a clear **Four years later — 2680** marker and Kiva's
  collegiate placement day, not a second origin scene.
- No snapshot may end with a line that functions as a thesis statement for
  the book ("and so the age of three doors began" or similar). Let the human
  beat carry the transition.
- The prologue's last unresolved question in the reader's mind should be
  ordinary and personal (what happens to this household today), not cosmic
  (what caused the Fall). The cosmic questions stay open, but they should not
  be the last thing the prologue foregrounds before Chapter 1.

## 7. Drafting choices resolved

1. Use the copied-reasons habit as the shared physical thread; reconcile exact
   postscript wording later without changing the prologue's independent meaning.
2. The author may use original, one-scene named figures in snapshots 1–5. They do
   not become continuing cast or hidden ancestors.
3. Tamar Vey is the locked historical name and function in Snapshot 6. The author
   owns his exact story, demonstration, humor, and dialogue while preserving the
   fuel-versus-instruction insight and his mortal, catalytic limits.
4. Use the planned eleven snapshots unless two adjacent scenes genuinely duplicate the
   same human choice. Do not collapse the energy revolution into one montage.
5. Snapshot 11 ends on the crooked post before Chapter One begins.

| Sat 13th, ~00:30–02:44 | Backwash fever; Unroofed Sky recurrence (unnamed); Kiva wakes with left palm on flat cell panel; Coper Street core trips 02:44; Mara quenches flat cell. | 7 |

Noon: Mara signs roster consent on the embankment with three household conditions. Evening: Rhea reviews run plate, writes draft card + private card. | 8 |

Trial run clock (Team Seven): Gate 0:00; first husk 0:30; Kiva declares tuning change ~1:45; three-husk release and three-door contamination ~2:25–2:40; Kiva over the rail **3:31 (undeclared)**; breaker trip **3:38**; Renn declares and quenches **3:44**; casualty over green line **4:41** (standard 4:00).

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan** (17). Provisional roster place, **Third Division, Crooked Line School, third team**; sponsor **O. Dane**. Tuition only, no stipend.
- Ysra's conditions: no live-cell events until sponsor certifies in writing; declare one door to the referee before every sanctioned event and declare any change; book to sponsor weekly; review after first sanctioned meet; one sanctioned-event incident ends her season.
- Mara's conditions: never touch any cell in the flat until Mara says; report the dream the same night; book on the kitchen table nightly and Mara reads it.
- Readings (Classification Day): Draw 3.1 (2.9 last year); Hold Edge 3.0, Ward 2.6, Stance 6.2 (5.8 last year); Rank unsettled (frame flickers Ember/Flame/dashes); First door none. Call column dark.
- Edge: square-ended, single-edged **bar**, forearm length; edge-light dull orange, gutters; cannot reshape. Discovered use: broad flat stops narrow blades dead. Ember slab: dull copper, leather cuff, **left** forearm. Stride: 3–4 bounds. Ward: tray-sized, splashes when dropped; learning to curve/fold. Stance: her steadiest door; learned "give the ground its weight back."
- Meter: bottom heat-line (nearest breastbone) warms first; she calls "one."
- Drill (open-close-open, not a true sequence): best 9.5 s. **No correct cross-door hand-off has ever succeeded.** Attempted Stride→stance hand-off in the Cellhouse produced a two-door stack (hand door held out).
- **Injuries:** left knee strained Wed 10th, re-strained Fri 12th (ice; don't land on it). Left forearm scraped raw wrist→elbow (husk limb). **Heat-lines now run from mid-breastbone over the collarbone, past the shoulder, halfway down the outer upper arm; raised, red, two blistered patches.** Arm in sling ~1 week. **Left first and second fingers:** numb after backwash; sensation returning but duller than the third finger ("thin gloves") — planted precursor only; not permanent loss yet. Nosebleed during fever. Healer Aske at the Wren House is the named follow-up.
- Knows: the Annick account? **No.** The name "Unroofed Sky"? **No.** Has copied the three T-0 register lines into her book; *Why: unknown.* Has now told Mara the dream in full for the first time.
- Disk: **cold throughout**, never warm. Swung out of her collar at ~3:39 in the Cellhouse and was visible on the run plate.

**Mara Rowan** (Kindled, hand door, Flame; Grade Two Tuning License). Burn scar across right palm and inside wrist (2677 Linden Row cell). **License: one further household filing triggers automatic review — the 13th's core trip is that filing; Mara filed it herself; Works review notice expected by end of week.** Signed trial consent (Wed 10th) and roster consent (Sat 13th), saying aloud that roster supervision may help her license review. Withheld the license letter from Kiva all winter and apologized for it. Has read the field book. Clerk at Hanley Street is hand-copying the fragile 2446 register entry for her.

**Orla Dane** (50s; Ember door, Edge, Torch; ex-Home Line field officer ~20 years; bad left knee, stick). Now Kiva's **accountable sponsor**: if Kiva has a sanctioned-event incident, Orla loses sponsoring standing and her teaching contract is reviewed. Private history now told to Kiva: sponsored **Jessamy Hart** (twin-kindled Ember + hand, Brickfields) in 2659; doors fought on a live cell before ~4,000 people; he lost a hand; two students burned; file sealed; left Meridian at nineteen; reportedly fixing pumps on the south coast; Orla never wrote. Standing suspended ten years, restored eleven years ago. Knew Ilse Corran; saw the disk on her at the Ferren levee ~2655. Has read the Annick notebook ("the sky unroofed") in the Compact Library; has told no one and has no proof it relates to Kiva.

**Anwen Pryce** (17; hand door, Ward + Sustain read; Flame; Hold 7.4, Draw 0.95; Wren House field track). Friends with Kiva since nine; her family runs the laundry on Kiva's building's ground floor (shared core). In the trial she chose the casualty over leaving and said so. On Graywater field officer **Doran Mace**'s list, "higher than I was." Graywater posts four field placements on the 20th. Knows Rhea Sorn asked for the run. Training at Wren House lanes; skipping Orla's Monday night; wants Thursday's drill. Private note: "I want it anyway. On my own."

**Perrin Cade** (17; still door, Stonehand Hall; Flame, upper band; Hold 6.8). Moved by board ruling to Team Seven (resented; said so). Held ~400 kg shutter on his hands for ~9 s after winch loss, then released by "giving it back"; individual note "exceptional." Hands purple, bandaged. **Stonehand bursary granted: Second Division, tuition and stipend.** Father (tram-yard brake mechanic) watched from the gallery. Grateful and still openly jealous. Asked to be told before being rescued.

**Kellan Renn** (17–18; Ember door, Fire; Hold 9.1, Draw 0.82; blue-grey slab on steel cuff; long narrow single-edged blade, blue-white edge-light). **First Division, Crooked Line first team.** Mother: ash grader at the Halden refinery for 22 years; a falsified stamp nearly blamed on her when he was nine; her written number saved her. Believes measurement protects people not in charge. Lost no points to anyone since autumn until Kiva's ½ off the riser. Paper notes on Kiva in his pocket. In the trial: held the stair, declared and took the quench from the rail; scraped right knee. Told the booth he trusted Kiva's numbers. "I'll be comparing."

**Ysra Crest** (~60; hand door, Torch; ex-Wren House ward-holder; Placement Chair, 19 years running the Cellhouse, 11 master-stop pulls). Kept her hand on the master stop from ~8.0 and did not pull it. Ruled provisional Third Division on the strength of the book, while stating the run failed.

**Rhea Sorn** (41; Graywater; Ember door, Edge only, Torch; 23 years; draft board six seasons; white streak at left temple; pale grey eyes). Reviewed Team Seven 3:30–end on the run plate Saturday evening. Draft card (in tray): *NOT for draft this season. Watch first sanctioned meet … Responder.* Private card (in her pocket, not filed): *Corran's disk.* At nineteen, seconded to a Home Line crew in the Ferren culvert floods, she was pulled out of a culvert by Ilse Corran and saw the disk then. Has not contacted Kiva.

**Ilse Corran** (prior bearer; d. 2673). Home Line field officer; tall, cropped grey hair, long jaw scar; friend of Ione Rowan; carried the disk ~30 years; appointed Kiva after Ione's death (2669), sealed until the Kindling day; delivered by a Home Line courier at 07:00 on the 2676 Kindling morning.

**Ione Rowan** (Kiva's mother, Mara's sister; d. 2669). Eastbank substation tech; Tallow substation runaway on cut ash stamped First warped the gate; she quenched by hand; the fire killed her.

Minor named: Brannock (rushing municipal operator, taught by Orla), Tobin (van driver), Mrs. Pryce (burn salve), Healer Aske (Wren House), Doran Mace (Graywater field officer).

## 3. World facts established on the page

- **Circuit axes:** Rank, Hold (out of ten), Draw (ash per standard work vs 1.0; lower is better), Call (team judgment; dark on Classification Day). Projection boards; draw bench (lamp-cell, sticky needle at "two"); hold posts; lit frame. No overlay.
- **Divisions:** First, Second, Third. Third = tuition only. Provisional placements carry a sponsor line.
- **Doors in 2680 speech:** Ember door / hand door / still door. Kindling stations: Ember slab in a brazier, bowl of rift-pool water (Wren House), stance stone; then the lit frame.
- **Open lane:** Stonehand's Terrace Ring — sunken bowl, three knee-high steps a pace and a half deep, ~12-pace raked sand floor, rim sensing mast, standard dram of First settled before bouts; dark Edge only; 1 point body/head, ½ limb; three wins.
- **Cellhouse:** ex-refinery, ~40 × 14 paces; Gate and green line west; mezzanine substation east (12-step south-wall stair, drum regulator, red quench with wire seal, quench box of wet salt, load board on the east wall, winch on the same circuit); pump room under the mezzanine with a jammed shutter (~400 kg); chute mid-south wall from **the Kennel**, a bottle-rift in the basement vault in a Wren House–maintained lattice impressed by a Glory master ~60 years ago. Trial husks are dog-sized, Handful-grade. Objectives 1–4 and violations as briefed by Ysra. Breaker trip at 8.5 = uncontrolled; breaker cuts load but **does not stop a pattern; only the quench does.**
- **Cells:** a cell keeps what you leave in it, including mood (Brannock's "hurry"). Kiva's three-door contamination persisted after release and **called husks** (rift attraction) — never before seen in the Compact.
- **Paper law:** every serious ash device has a chained paper cell book; the *Transient Register* records unexplained flickers. **T-0** = transient, no cause. Three multi-grid T-0s: **2446** (night, every lamp, every district; fragile volume, copy pending), **2604** (03:10, Compact Quarter; margin: "Again. Same as the '46 entry in the old book. Every lamp at once."), **2676** (14:52, Eastbank + Lantern + Weighbridge grids; Kiva's Kindling). Last night's trip was **local only** (one shared core).
- **Sag Line masts:** baselines creeping upward on every mast for years; recalibration masks it; Mara declined to recalibrate mast six. Measurement only — no theory confirmed.
- **Institutions/colours:** Graywater (slate blue, white wave; hall on the Brickfields road), Saltmere (green), Halden companies (black and gold); Order of the Even Hand (Ward devotion). City banner: *SIX HUNDRED YEARS. WE WROTE IT DOWN.*
- **The crooked post:** last post in the Old Yard lane, black, split, two iron bands, leans left. Standard manual: "retained for ceremonial continuity" (since the 2603 committee vote, 11–6). Orla: "There used to be one. It's not in the manual anymore." Marisol's pencil note survives in the superseded stacks.

## 4. Prologue canon touched (for owner review)

- No present-trilogy figure is dramatized. Snapshot 4 shows initials only (T.V., J., D.W., K., S.V.) inside a copied ledger.
- Failing-filter idea appears first as post-Homura hearsay in 2152 ("the sky had a lid… the bombs cracked the lid"), never confirmed.
- Snapshot 4 is dated **2152**, slightly past the plan's ~2135–2150 range, so the copy can follow the Book Three postscript's copying. Flag if the owner wants it pulled inside 2150.
- Tamar Vey: large, slow, limping former Home Line ward-holder (hand door), letters-and-reasons teacher; the weir story and the bowl/copper/stone/ash demonstration (fence holds ~50 breaths after release, until the pinch is spent). He leaves; Gideon never sees him again. Gideon's question entry is the only record.
- Corbel Reach: the 2081 meltdown (diesel ran out in the second winter after the Fall); 2170 radiation return; 2195 Unit One with a 48-cartridge ash lattice set by six Kindled and checked by a Blaze ward master (no Glory figure onstage); cartridge 31 failure → "Check the pairs" opens every later shutdown code; ran 19 years.
- 2247: Halden-licensed Class C domestic core; master pattern impressed by an unseen Glory master.
- **Hesper Annick:** Even Hand master, Glory 2431, the flood-wall ward, walked out in the winter rifts of 2461, recorded dead by the Order; notebook c. 2446–2450 with the fever account and the phrase "the sky unroofed." **The register's 2446 T-0 was placed deliberately to sit inside that span.** Neither Kiva nor Mara knows Annick exists. Flag for review.
- The Kindling (2676): disk delivered that morning; Ember opens at the brazier (slab turns dull copper), hand door at the bowl (water rises), still door at the stone; frame cannot settle; three heat-lines appear; the dream comes while the disk goes "colder"; yard lamps blink; "Classification deferred … There isn't a column for this."

|---|---|
| Disk warms only after first correct deliberate sequence | Never warm. Orla's definition now on the page: a sequence passes one door's work into the next without dropping it; the drill is not a sequence. |
| Crooked post's reason | Retired 2603; Orla knows it once existed; Marisol's pencil note survives. |
| Sag Line baseline creep | Recorded, unexplained. |
| T-0 entries (2446/2604/2676) | Copied by Kiva; 2446 copy pending from the clerk. |

| Rhea's "Corran's disk" card | Private; unrevealed. |
| Mara's license review | Notice due end of week; outcome open. |
| Anwen's Graywater placement | Decided on the 20th. |
| Kellan's comparison | First Division vs Kiva's Third. |
| Two-finger sensation | Duller, not lost. Permanent loss remains reserved for Movement 5. |
| Jessamy Hart | Alive, south coast, uncontacted. |
| Cell contamination as a three-door failure mode | Demonstrated once; Compact has no procedure for it. |

## 6. Deliberately not done

The disk never warms, speaks, or teaches. No correct cross-door hand-off succeeds. No rank is revealed. The Unroofed Sky is not named to Kiva and supplies no tactic. The filter, the Makers, the disk's origin, and the cause of the Fall stay unresolved. Noa Bexley and Hollis Vane do not appear.
